<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We face competition from companies that have greater resources than we do and we may not be able to effectively compete against these companies.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We operate in a highly competitive industry.&#160; Our primary competitors in the mobile rugged computer market include Panasonic, Getac, DRS Technologies and Mobile Demand in the tablet PC market and Panasonic in the notebook market.&#160; We also face competition from manufacturers of non-rugged mobile computers, such as Samsung, Inc., Dell, Inc., Hewlett-Packard Company, Apple, Inc., Sony and Toshiba, to the extent customers decide to purchase less expensive traditional computers for use in environments that we believe are better suited for mobile rugged computers. The principal competitive factors in our industry include:</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">
<div>
<table style="WIDTH: 100%; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt" id="z406f9178851742709bc8fcf5bf91faaf" class="DSPFListTable" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0">
<tr>
<td style="WIDTH: 72pt; VERTICAL-ALIGN: top; align: right">

</td>
<td style="WIDTH: auto; VERTICAL-ALIGN: top">
<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">product performance, features and reliability;</div>
</td>

<div>
<table style="WIDTH: 100%; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt" id="z923aabb79bae46ef83bfd8235dff04da" class="DSPFListTable" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0">

<div>
<table style="WIDTH: 100%; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt" id="zd677fa7c43db4334ab19a49ba6f3216f" class="DSPFListTable" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0">
<tr>
<td style="WIDTH: 72pt; VERTICAL-ALIGN: top; align: right">
<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-FAMILY: Symbol, serif; MARGIN-LEFT: 54pt; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">&#183;</div>
</td>
<td style="WIDTH: auto; VERTICAL-ALIGN: top">
<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">name recognition; and</div>
</td>
</tr>
</table>
</div>

<div>
<table style="WIDTH: 100%; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt" id="z3cc8dcc1dd2c4b239e9ac4c83efc77b7" class="DSPFListTable" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0">
<tr>
<td style="WIDTH: 72pt; VERTICAL-ALIGN: top; align: right">
<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-FAMILY: Symbol, serif; MARGIN-LEFT: 54pt; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">&#183;</div>
</td>
<td style="WIDTH: auto; VERTICAL-ALIGN: top">
<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">product availability and lead times.</div>
</td>
</tr>
</table>
</div>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Most of our competitors have a wider variety of products, longer operating histories, greater name recognition, larger customer bases and significantly greater financial, technical, sales, marketing and other resources than we do.&#160; In addition, because of the higher volume of components that many of our competitors purchase from their suppliers, they are able to keep their costs of supply relatively low and, as a result, may be able to recognize higher margins on their product sales than we do.&#160; Many of our competitors may also have existing relationships with our contract manufacturers, the resellers who we use to sell our products, or with our potential customers.&#160; This competition may result in reduced prices, reduced margins and longer sales cycles for our products.&#160; The introduction of lower-priced personal computers, combined with the brand strength, more product offerings to address shifting demand for specific rugged product form factors, extensive distribution channels and financial resources of the larger vendors, could cause us to lose market share and could reduce our margins on those personal computers we sell.&#160; If any of our larger competitors were to commit greater technical, sales, marketing and other resources to our markets, our ability to compete would be adversely affected.&#160; If we are unable to successfully compete with our competitors our sales would suffer and as a result our financial condition will be adversely affected.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">If we are unable to acquire key components or are unable to acquire them on favorable terms, our business will suffer.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Some key components included in our line of products are currently available only from single or limited sources.&#160;&#160;In addition, some of the suppliers of these components are also supplying certain of our competitors.&#160;&#160;We cannot be certain that our suppliers will be able to meet our demand for components in a timely and cost-effective manner.&#160;&#160;We carry little inventory of our products or our product components and, as a result, rely on our suppliers to deliver our products and necessary components to us or our contract manufacturers in a timely manner based upon forecasts we provide.&#160;&#160;If any of our suppliers become unreliable in providing components, we may not be able to develop an alternative source of supply in a timely manner, which could hurt our ability to deliver our products to our customers.&#160;&#160;In addition, if we are unable to buy these components on a timely and a cost-efficient basis, we may not be able to deliver products to our customers, or the margins we receive for our products may suffer, which would negatively impact our future financial performance and, in turn, seriously harm our business.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">At various times, some of the key components for our products have been in short supply.&#160;&#160;Delays in receiving components would harm our ability to deliver our products on a timely basis.&#160;&#160;In addition, because we expect to rely on purchase orders rather than long-term contracts with our suppliers, we cannot predict with certainty our ability to procure components in the longer term.&#160;&#160;If we receive a smaller allocation of components than is necessary to manufacture our products in quantities sufficient to meet our customers&#8217; demand, those customers could choose to purchase competing products.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In January 2015, the supplier of screens for our iX104C6 product went out of business without giving us any prior notice.&#160; In addition, the same supplier supplied screens for approximately 50% of Motion&#8217;s products.&#160; We identified a replacement supplier for this component to continue producing our iX104C6 product, but we were not able to finish the transition to the new component in time to avoid delays in shipping our iX104C6 product.&#160; We also transitioned to a new supplier of screens for the Motion C5 and F5 products.&#160; We suffered delays in shipping some of our iX104C6 product orders as well as some of the Motion C5 and F5 product orders in the first quarter of our fiscal year ending March 31, 2016, some of which may have caused the loss of sales in the that fiscal year and beyond, which could harm our business, financial condition and results of operations.&#160; We continue to have to manage our suppliers of screens for our products, and may experience additional disruptions in the future.&#160; In addition, the delays in shipping shifted some of our revenues into later periods, which may make it even more difficult for you to rely on quarter-to-quarter comparisons of our results of operations as an indicator of our future results.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 10pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 10pt; CLEAR: both" id="DSPFPageBreakArea">

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">If we are unable to successfully protect our intellectual property, our competitive position will be harmed.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our ability to compete is heavily affected by our ability to protect our intellectual property.&#160; We rely on a combination of patents, copyright and trademark laws, trade secret, confidentiality procedures and contractual provisions to protect our proprietary rights.&#160; We also enter, and plan to continue to enter, into confidentiality or license agreements with our employees, consultants and other parties with whom we contract, and control access to and distribution of our software, documentation and other proprietary information.&#160; The steps we take to protect our technology may be inadequate.&#160; Existing trade secret, trademark and copyright laws offer only limited protection.&#160; Unauthorized parties may attempt to copy aspects of our products or obtain and use information that we regard as proprietary.&#160; Policing unauthorized use of our products is difficult, time consuming and costly, particularly in foreign countries where the laws may not protect our proprietary rights as fully as in the United States.&#160; We cannot assure you that our means of protecting our proprietary rights will be adequate or that our competitors will not independently develop similar technology, the effect of either of which would harm our competitive position in the market.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Others could claim that we infringe on their intellectual property rights, which may result in costly and time consuming litigation and could delay or otherwise impair the development and commercialization of our products.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In recent years, there has been a significant increase in litigation in the United States involving patents and other intellectual property rights.&#160; We do not believe that our products infringe on the proprietary rights of third parties.&#160; There can be no assurance, however, that third parties will not claim such infringement by us or our licensees with respect to current or future products.&#160; Claims for alleged infringement and any resulting lawsuit, if successful, could subject us to significant liability for damages and invalidation of our intellectual property rights.&#160; Any such claims, with or without merit, could be time consuming, expensive to defend, cause product shipment delays or require us to enter into royalty or licensing agreements, any of which could delay the development and commercialization of our products or reduce our margins.&#160; If we are unable to obtain a required license, our ability to sell or use certain products may be impaired.&#160; In addition, if we fail to obtain a license, or if the terms of the license are burdensome to us, our operations could be materially harmed.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We are subject to risks by doing business outside of the United States that could impair our ability to grow our revenues.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In the fiscal years ended March&#160;31, 2017 and March&#160;31, 2016, approximately 27% and 32%, respectively, of our revenue was comprised of sales made outside of the United States.&#160; In the calendar year 2014, approximately 46% of Motion&#8217;s revenue was comprised of sales made outside of the United States, much of which was denominated in as many as six foreign currencies.&#160; Our future business and financial performance could suffer due to a variety of international factors, including:</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">The factors described above also could disrupt our product and component manufacturing and key suppliers located outside of the United States.&#160; For example, we rely on manufacturers in Taiwan for the production of our tablet computers.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In many foreign countries, particularly in those with developing economies, there are companies that engage in business practices prohibited by laws and regulations applicable to us, such as the Foreign Corrupt Practices Act of 1977, as amended, or the FCPA.&#160; Although we are implementing policies, procedures and training designed to facilitate compliance with these laws, our employees, contractors and agents may take actions in violation of these policies.&#160; Any such violation, even if prohibited by our policies, could have an adverse effect on our business and reputation.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">As we continue expanding our cross-border operations into foreign markets, we will face risks associated with expanding into markets in which we have limited or no experience and in which we may be less well-known.&#160; We may be unable to attract a sufficient number of customers, fail to anticipate competitive conditions or face difficulties in operating effectively in these new markets or across markets.&#160; The expansion of our international business will also expose us to risks relating to staffing and managing those operations, increases in duty rates, exchange or price controls, increased costs to protect intellectual property, tariffs and other trade barriers, differing and potentially adverse tax consequences, increased and conflicting regulatory compliance requirements, political, social and economic changes and disruptions, lack of acceptance of our product offerings, challenges caused by distance, language and cultural differences and possible difficulty in enforcing contracts or legal rights under foreign legal systems.&#160; Accordingly, any efforts we make to expand our international operations may not be successful, which would have a material and adverse effect on our business, financial condition and results of operations.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">If we are unable to retain key personnel, we may not be able to execute our business strategy.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; MARGIN: 3pt 0.1pt 6pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our operations are dependent on the abilities, experience and efforts of a number of key personnel, including Mark Holleran, our president and chief executive officer and Tom Wilkinson, our chief financial officer and corporate secretary.&#160; Should either of these persons or other key employees be unable or unwilling to continue in our employ, our ability to execute our business strategy may be adversely effected.&#160; In addition, our success is highly dependent on our continuing ability to identify, hire, train, motivate and retain highly qualified management, technical and sales and marketing personnel.&#160;&#160;Competition for such personnel is intense.&#160;&#160;We may be unable to attract and retain the personnel necessary for the development of our business.&#160;&#160;Because we have experienced operating losses, which resulted in a reduction in our workforce in the past, we may have a more difficult time in attracting and retaining the employees we need.&#160; Other than an employment agreement with our president and chief executive officer, our relationships with our key employees are &#8220;at will.&#8221;&#160;&#160;Also, we do not have &#8220;key person&#8221; life insurance policies covering any of our employees.&#160;&#160;The inability to attract or retain qualified personnel in the future or delays in hiring skilled personnel could harm our relations with our customers and our ability to respond to technological change, which could prevent us from executing our business strategy.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We will depend on the continued performance of certain members of our senior management team and certain other key employees to assist in executing our strategy after the Motion Acquisition.&#160; We also expect that our efforts to integrate the Motion product lines will place a significant strain on our personnel, management systems, infrastructure and other resources.&#160; Our ability to manage future growth effectively will also require us to successfully attract, train, motivate, retain and manage new employees and continue to update and improve our operational, financial and management controls and procedures.&#160;</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">If we fail to predict our manufacturing requirements accurately, we could incur additional costs or experience manufacturing delays, which could reduce our gross margins or cause us to lose sales.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We provide, and will continue to provide, forecasts of our demand to our contract manufacturers prior to the scheduled delivery of products to our customers.&#160;&#160;If we overestimate our requirements, our contract manufacturers may have excess component inventory, which could increase our costs.&#160;&#160;If we underestimate our requirements, our contract manufacturers may have an inadequate component inventory, which could interrupt the manufacturing of our products and result in delays in shipments and revenue.&#160;&#160;In addition, lead times for materials and components that we order vary significantly and depend on factors such as the specific supplier, contract terms and demand for each component at a given time.&#160;&#160;We may also experience shortages of components from time to time, which also could delay the manufacturing of our products or increase the costs of our products.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our inability to obtain any third-party license required to develop new products and product enhancements could seriously harm our business, financial condition and results of operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">From time to time, we are required to license technology from third parties to develop new products or product enhancements.&#160;&#160;Third-party licenses may not be available to us on commercially reasonable terms, or at all.&#160;&#160;Our inability to obtain any third-party license necessary to develop new products or product enhancements could require us to obtain substitute technology of lower quality or performance standards, or at greater cost, which could seriously harm our business, financial condition and results of operations.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We must respond quickly and effectively to new technological developments, and the failure to do so could have a material and adverse effect on our results of operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our failure to maintain our technological capabilities or to respond effectively to technological changes could adversely affect our business, results of operations or financial condition.&#160; Our future success also depends on our ability to enhance existing hardware and systems and to respond to changing technological developments.&#160; If we are unable to successfully develop and bring to market new hardware and systems in a timely manner, our competitors&#8217; technologies or services may render our products or services noncompetitive or obsolete.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Security breaches and other disruptions could compromise our information and expose us to liability, which would cause our business and reputation to suffer.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In the ordinary course of our business, we store sensitive data, including intellectual property, our proprietary business information and that of our customers and suppliers, and personally identifiable information of our customers and employees, in our data centers and on our networks.&#160; The secure maintenance of this information is critical to our operations.&#160; Despite our security measures, our information technology and infrastructure may be vulnerable to attacks by hackers or breached due to employee error, malfeasance or other disruptions.&#160; Any such breach could compromise our networks and the information stored there could be accessed, publicly disclosed, lost or stolen.&#160; Any such access, disclosure or other loss of information could result in legal claims or proceedings, disrupt our operations, damage our reputation, and cause a loss of confidence in our products and services, which could adversely affect our business, revenues and competitive position.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our planned implementation of an ERP software solution and other information technology systems could result in significant disruptions to our operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We plan to implement an enterprise resource planning, or ERP, and other complementary information technology systems over the next several years.&#160; Implementation of these solutions and systems is highly dependent on coordination of numerous software and system providers and internal business teams.&#160; The interdependence of these solutions and systems is a significant risk to the successful completion of the initiatives and the failure of any one system could have a material adverse effect on the implementation of our overall information technology infrastructure.&#160; We may experience difficulties as we transition to these new or upgraded systems and processes, including loss or corruption of data, delayed shipments, decreases in productivity as our personnel and third party providers implement and become familiar with new systems, increased costs and lost revenues.&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In addition, transitioning to these new systems requires significant capital investments and personnel resources.&#160; Difficulties in implementing new or upgraded information systems or significant system failures could disrupt our operations and have a material adverse effect on our capital resources, financial condition, results of operations or cash flows.&#160; Implementation of this new information technology infrastructure has a significant impact on our business processes and information systems across a significant portion of our operations.&#160; As a result, we will be undergoing significant changes in our operational processes and internal controls as our implementation progresses, which in turn will require significant change management, including recruiting and training of qualified personnel.&#160; If we are unable to successfully manage these changes as we implement these systems, including harmonizing our systems, data, processes and reporting analytics, our ability to conduct, manage and control routine business functions could be negatively affected and significant disruptions to our business could occur.&#160; In addition, we could incur material unanticipated expenses, including additional costs of implementation or costs of conducting business.&#160; These risks could result in significant business disruptions or divert management&#8217;s attention from key strategic initiatives and have a material adverse effect on our capital resources, financial condition, results of operations or cash flows.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Risks Relating to Ownership of our Common Stock</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">The anti-takeover effect of certain of our recently adopted rights plan and certain of our charter provisions could adversely affect holders of our common stock.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">On July 1, 2016, in connection with the adoption of a stockholder rights plan, our board of directors declared a dividend of one right for each of our issued and outstanding shares of common stock.&#160; The dividend was paid to stockholders of record at the close of business on July 15, 2016.&#160; Each such right entitles the registered holder, subject to the terms of a rights agreement, to purchase from us one one-thousandth of a share of our Series E Participating Preferred Stock.&#160; The purpose of the rights plan is to diminish the risk that our ability to use our significant tax benefits to reduce potential future federal income tax obligations would become subject to limitations by reason of us experiencing an &#8220;ownership change,&#8221; as defined in Section 382 of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986.&#160; However, the rights plan makes it more difficult for a third party to acquire 5% or more of the voting power of our voting securities without the cooperation of our board of directors, which could deprive holders of our common stock of a premium that they might otherwise realize in connection with the acquisition of us by a third party.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In addition, our authorized capital consists of preferred stock issuable in one or more series.&#160; Our board of directors has the authority to issue preferred stock and determine the price, designation, rights, preferences, privileges, restrictions and conditions, including voting and dividend rights, of those shares without any further vote or action by stockholders.&#160; The rights of the holders of common stock will be subject to, and may be adversely affected by, the rights of holders of any preferred stock that may be issued in the future.&#160; The issuance of additional preferred stock, while providing desirable flexibility in connection with possible financings and acquisitions and other corporate purposes, could make it more difficult for a third party to acquire a majority of the voting power of our outstanding voting securities, which could also deprive holders of our common stock of a premium that they might otherwise realize in connection with the acquisition of us by a third party.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">As of June 14, 2017, Phoenix Venture Fund&#160;LLC and its affiliates beneficially own approximately 14.8% of our common stock, and thus have significant influence over matters requiring stockholder approval.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">One of our stockholders, Phoenix Venture Fund&#160;LLC, or Phoenix, which is co-managed by Philip S. Sassower, our chairman of the board, and Andrea Goren, one of our directors, beneficially owns approximately 9.9% of our common stock.&#160;&#160;In addition, Mr.&#160;Sassower, personally and through other entities controlled by him other than Phoenix, beneficially owns, in the aggregate, approximately 4.6% of our common stock, and Mr. Goren, personally and through other entities controlled by him other than Phoenix, beneficially owns, in the aggregate, approximately 0.5% of our common stock.&#160; Thus, Phoenix, Mr.&#160;Sassower and Mr. Goren, together, beneficially own in the aggregate, approximately 14.8% of our common stock.&#160;&#160;Accordingly, Phoenix, Mr.&#160;Sassower and Mr. Goren have the ability to exercise significant influence over matters generally requiring stockholder approval, including the election of directors and the approval of significant corporate transactions, which could have the effect of delaying or preventing a third party from acquiring control over us.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We do not expect to pay dividends on our common stock.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We have never paid cash dividends on our common stock.&#160; Our current policy is to retain any future earnings to finance the future development and expansion of our business.&#160; Any future determination about the payment of dividends will be made at the discretion of our board of directors and will depend upon our earnings, capital requirements, operating and financial conditions and on such other factors the board of directors deems relevant.&#160; Under the terms of our financing agreement with our senior lender, we are prohibited from paying cash dividends to holders of our common stock.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold"><a name="Item1B.Unresolved"><!--Anchor--></a>Item&#160;1B.&#160; Unresolved Staff Comments</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><font style="FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">&#160;</font><font style="WIDTH: 36pt; DISPLAY: inline-block; FONT-SIZE: 1px" id="TRGRRTFtoHTMLTab">&#160;</font><font style="FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">None.</font><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold"><a name="Item2.Properties"><!--Anchor--></a>Item&#160;2.&#160; Properties</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We maintain our corporate functions, along with sales support, marketing and finance at a leased facility totaling approximately 16,228 square feet at 8601 Ranch Road 2222, Building II, Austin, Texas 78730.&#160; This lease expires on June 30, 2020 and has a current annual base rent, before reimbursable operating expenses, of approximately $235,000.&#160; We maintain our engineering and operating groups at a leased facility totaling 21,700 square feet at 14000 Summit Drive, Suite&#160;900, Austin, Texas, 78730.&#160; This lease expires on August&#160;31, 2019, and has a current annual base rent, before reimbursable operating expenses, of approximately $228,000.&#160; After the Motion Acquisition, during a portion of fiscal year 2016 we occupied, with the consent of the landlord, a portion of Motion&#8217;s former leased facility at 8601 Ranch Road 2222, where Motion maintained its corporate functions, along with sales support, marketing, finance, engineering and operating groups.&#160; We did not assume Motion&#8217;s lease for the facility, and in fiscal year 2016 negotiated the terms of a new lease with the landlord, as described above, for a smaller portion of Motion&#8217;s former facility.&#160; We believe that our present facilities are suitable for our existing and planned operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold"><a name="Item3.Legal"><!--Anchor--></a>Item&#160;3.&#160; Legal Proceedings</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We are not currently involved in any other legal actions, arising in the ordinary course of business or otherwise.&#160; From time to time, we may be involved in various claims and legal actions arising in the ordinary course of business.&#160; We believe that the ultimate outcome of these matters will not have a material adverse impact on our financial condition or results of operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold"><a name="Item4.Mine"><!--Anchor--></a>Item&#160;4.&#160; Mine Safety Disclosures</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Not applicable.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: center; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">PART II</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold"><a name="Item5.Marketfor"><!--Anchor--></a>Item&#160;5.&#160;&#160; Market for Registrant&#8217;s Common Equity, Related Stockholder Matters and Issuer Purchases of Equity Securities.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Market Information</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our common stock is listed on The NASDAQ Stock Market under the symbol &#8220;XPLR.&#8221;&#160; As of June 12, 2017, there were 243 registered holders of record of our common stock, and the closing price of our common stock on The NASDAQ Stock Market was $1.86.&#160;&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">The following table sets forth the high and low sales price of our common stock on The NASDAQ Stock Market for each quarter period during the fiscal years ended March 31, 2017 and 2016, in U.S. dollars.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We have not declared or paid any dividends on our common stock during our last five fiscal years.&#160; The payment of dividends on our common stock in the future will depend on our earnings, capital requirements, operating and financial condition and such other factors as our board of directors may consider appropriate.&#160; Under the terms of our financing agreement with our senior lender, we are prohibited from paying cash dividends without the senior lender&#8217;s prior written consent.&#160; We currently expect to use all available funds to finance the future development and expansion of our business and do not anticipate paying dividends on our common stock in the foreseeable future.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Recent Sales of Unregistered Securities</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">You should read the following discussion and analysis in conjunction with our consolidated financial statements and notes included in this Annual Report on Form 10-K.&#160; This discussion contains, in addition to historical information, forward&#8209;looking statements that involve risks and uncertainty.&#160; Our actual results could differ materially from those anticipated in the forward&#8209;looking statements, including those discussed in &#8220;Risk Factors&#8221; and elsewhere in this Annual Report on Form 10-K.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">General</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">&#160;We are engaged in the development, integration and marketing of rugged mobile personal computer systems, or PCs. Our rugged tablet PCs are designed to withstand hazardous conditions, such as extreme temperatures, driving rain, repeated vibrations, dirt, dust and concussive shocks.&#160; The intrinsically safe, ruggedized and reliable nature of our products facilitates the extension of traditional computing systems to a broader range of field personnel, including energy pipeline inspectors, public safety responders, warehouse workers and pharmaceutical scientists. Our tablets are fitted with a range of performance-matched accessories, including multiple docking solutions, wireless connectivity alternatives, global positioning system modules, biometric and smartcard options, as well as traditional peripherals, such as keyboards and cases.&#160; Additionally, our most rugged tablets are waterproof for up to 30 minutes in water up to a depth of three feet, are impervious to drops from as high as seven feet, are readable in direct sunlight, can be mounted on vehicles and include LTE and Wi-Fi connectivity options for real-time data access.&#160; Our end user customers include major telecommunications companies, leading heavy equipment manufacturers, oil and gas production companies, the military and first responders.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Until 2014, we competed and derived our revenue through the sale of our iX104 tablets in a subset of the rugged PC market, given the larger size and ultra-rugged attributes of our iX104 tablet, which weighs approximately 5.4 pounds.&#160; We have reduced our dependency upon the continued market acceptance of our iX104 systems, by broadening the market for our products and increasing our opportunities for revenue growth.&#160; We have broadened our product offering, through development and acquisition, to include multiple fully-rugged and rugged tablets that are lighter weight and less expensive than our iX104 line of products.&#160; These new products have allowed us to compete in significantly larger segments of the rugged PC market.&#160; In 2014, we introduced our first fully-rugged, lighter weight tablets, offering Android and Windows operating systems. In 2015 we introduced an additional fully-rugged, lighter weight Windows tablet with greater processing speed, and we completed the acquisition of certain assets of Motion Computing, Inc. and its subsidiaries, which we refer to herein as Motion, more fully discussed below.&#160; This acquisition, which we refer to herein as the Motion Acquisition, expanded our product lines to include multiple additional rugged tablet products, and expanded our domestic and international customer base.&#160; We believe the lighter and less expensive tablets are ideal for field service applications and significantly more mobile market opportunities, as compared to our iX104.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">The timing of large orders, and the related shipping dates of the ordered products, creates variability in our reported revenues.&#160; While we may experience some variability in our quarterly operating results as a consequence of the impact of large orders, and we experienced a surge in non-recurring orders as a result of the Motion Acquisition, our goal is to continue to grow our year-over-year revenues.&#160; Our revenue for the fiscal year ended March 31, 2017, however, was approximately 22% lower than the prior fiscal year.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">On April 17, 2015, we completed the Motion Acquisition.&#160; Pursuant to the terms of a Foreclosure Purchase and Sale Agreement with Motion and Square 1 Bank, Motion&#8217;s senior secured lender, our subsidiary, Xplore Technologies Corporation of America, purchased certain of the Motion assets, including cash, cash equivalents, accounts receivable, inventory, equipment, personal property and other assets.&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Looking forward, our strategy is to build increased marketplace awareness of our products; an effort that we believe will enable us to increase our revenue and to expand our market share.&#160; We also expect to significantly increase our revenue and substantially expand our total market share through the addition of our new product lines, including those that we recently acquired in the Motion Acquisition.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Critical Accounting Policies</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our consolidated financial statements and accompanying notes included in this Annual Report on Form 10-K are prepared in accordance with U.S. generally accepted accounting principles.&#160; Preparing financial statements requires management to make estimates and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of assets, liabilities, revenue and expenses, and related disclosure of contingent assets and liabilities.&#160; These estimates and assumptions are affected by management&#8217;s application of accounting policies.&#160; Estimates are deemed critical when a different estimate could have reasonably been used or where changes in the estimates are reasonably likely to occur from period to period, and would materially impact our financial condition, changes in financial condition or results of operations.&#160; Our significant accounting policies are discussed in Note&#160;2 of the Notes to our Annual Consolidated Financial Statements included in this Annual Report on Form 10-K.&#160;</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 10pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 10pt; CLEAR: both" id="DSPFPageBreakArea">
<div id="DSPFPageFooter"></div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: center" id="DSPFPageNumberArea"><font style="FONT-STYLE: normal; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-WEIGHT: normal" id="DSPFPageNumber">23</font></div>

<div style="PAGE-BREAK-AFTER: always" id="DSPFPageBreak">
<hr style="BORDER-RIGHT-WIDTH: 0px; BACKGROUND-COLOR: #000000; MARGIN: 4px 0px; WIDTH: 100%; BORDER-TOP-WIDTH: 0px; BORDER-BOTTOM-WIDTH: 0px; HEIGHT: 2px; COLOR: #000000; CLEAR: both; BORDER-LEFT-WIDTH: 0px" noshade="noshade">

<div style="WIDTH: 100%" id="DSPFPageHeader">
<div><a href="#TableofContents">Table of Contents<br>
</a></div>

</div>

<div><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">On an ongoing basis, we evaluate our estimates, including those related to our revenue recognition, allowance for doubtful accounts, inventory valuation, warranty reserves, tooling amortization, financial instruments, stock based compensation and income taxes.&#160; We base our estimates on historical experience and on various other assumptions that we believe are reasonable under the circumstances, the results of which form the basis for making judgments about the carrying values of assets and liabilities that are not readily apparent from other sources.&#160; Actual results may differ from these estimates.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our critical accounting policies are as follows:</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Revenue Recognition.</font>&#160; Our revenue is derived from the sale of rugged mobile technology, which includes rugged mobile tablet PC computers and related accessories.&#160; Our customers are predominantly distributors and resellers.&#160; However, we also sell directly to end-users.&#160; We recognize revenue, net of an allowance for estimated returns, when title and risks of ownership are transferred to the customer, all significant contractual obligations have been satisfied, the sales price is fixed or determinable and the ability to collect is reasonably assured.&#160; Our revenue recognition criteria are met when either the product has been shipped, or when it has been received, depending upon agreed shipping terms.&#160; Shipments are based on firm purchase orders from our customers with stated terms. We do not have installation, training or other commitments subsequent to shipment that are other than incidental.&#160; Our prices are determined based on negotiations with our customers and are not subject to adjustment.&#160; Generally, we do not hold inventory at our resellers and we do not expect resellers to hold inventories of our products other than in limited circumstances in which such inventory is monitored by us.&#160; Our distributors do hold small amounts of inventory which we monitor.&#160; This inventory is held in order to more quickly respond to their customer demand.&#160; As a result of the low amount of reseller and distributor inventory, we expect returns to be minimal.&#160; We have not had material adjustments, as our returns have been minimal.&#160; Revenue from separately priced extended warranty contracts is deferred and recognized in income on a straight-line basis over the related contract period.&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Allowance for Doubtful Accounts. </font>We regularly review and monitor collections of our accounts receivables and make estimated provisions, generally monthly, based on our experience, aging attributes, results of collection efforts and current market conditions.&#160; If our estimate for allowance for doubtful accounts is too low, additional charges will be incurred in future periods, which additional charges could have a material adverse effect on our financial position and results of operations.&#160; Historically, our estimates have not required significant adjustment due to actual experience of very small amount of doubtful accounts.&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 39pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Warranty Reserves.</font>&#160; We make provisions for warranties at the time of sale, which are based on our experience and monitored regularly.&#160; The revenue related to warranty is recognized when our obligations are generally covered by a warranty coverage agreement provided by a third party.&#160; A portion of our warranty obligations related to revenue recognized are primarily covered by warranty coverage agreements provided by our contract manufacturers; however, we also provide the coverage on the portion of our obligations that is not covered by these agreements, for which we establish related reserves at the time of sale.&#160; We had been moving to more of a self-insured model due to changes in our warranty offerings and warranty claim experience. If our estimates for warranties and returns are too low, we will incur additional charges in future periods and these additional charges could have a material adverse effect on our financial position and results of operations.&#160; Historically, our estimates have not required significant adjustment due to actual experience.&#160;However, during 2017, we began purchasing warranty coverage to cover the basic warranty on all units sold.&#160; It is expected that beginning in fiscal year 2018, all new units will have basic warranties covered by warranties purchased from our contract manufacturers.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Inventory Valuation.</font>&#160; We adjust our inventory values so that the carrying value does not exceed net realizable value.&#160; The valuation of inventory at the lower of average cost or net realizable value requires us to use estimates regarding the amount of current inventory that will be sold and the prices at which it will be sold, and our assessment of expected orders from our customers.&#160; Additionally, the estimates reflect changes in our products or changes in demand because of various factors, including the market for our products, obsolescence, production discontinuation, minimum order quantities, technology changes and competition.&#160; While the estimates are subject to revisions and actual results could differ materially, historically our estimates have not required significant adjustment due to actual experience.&#160;</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Tooling Amortization.</font>&#160; We amortize tooling costs over a two-year period or estimated life, whichever is shorter.&#160; Those costs are recorded as a cost of revenue, subject to an assessment that future revenue will be sufficient to fully recover the cost of the tooling.&#160; This assessment requires an assessment of the market for our products and our future revenue expectations.&#160; On a quarterly basis, this assessment is reviewed and the cost of tooling is written down to its net realizable value if its recoverability is not reasonably expected based on estimates of future revenue.&#160; Historically, there have been no instances in which we determined that useful life was significantly less than two years.&#160; Accordingly, we have not recorded material adjustments.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 10pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 10pt; CLEAR: both" id="DSPFPageBreakArea">
<div id="DSPFPageFooter"></div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: center" id="DSPFPageNumberArea"><font style="FONT-STYLE: normal; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-WEIGHT: normal" id="DSPFPageNumber">24</font></div>

<div><br>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Goodwill and Other Intangible Assets.</font>&#160; Goodwill represents the excess purchase price over the fair value of identifiable assets received attributable to business acquisitions and combinations. Goodwill and other intangible assets are measured for impairment at least annually and/or whenever events and circumstances arise that indicate impairment may exist, such as a significant adverse change in the business climate.&#160; In assessing the value of goodwill, assets and liabilities are assigned to the reporting units and the appropriate valuation methodologies are used to determine fair value at the reporting unit level.&#160; Identified intangible assets are amortized using the straight-line method over their estimated useful lives, which are estimated to be between three and seven years.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Long-lived asset impairment.</font>&#160; Long-lived assets include property and equipment and definite-lived intangible assets. Definite-lived intangible assets consist of customer relationships, trade names and non-compete agreements. Long-lived assets are measured for impairment at least annually and/or whenever events and circumstances arise that indicate that the carrying value of the assets may not be recoverable. As a result of our decline in revenue and stock price, we made an assessment of impairment at March 31, 2017.&#160; Based on that assessment, no impairment had occurred.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Income Taxes.</font>&#160; We have a significant valuation allowance that we intend to maintain until it is more likely than not that our deferred tax assets will be realized.&#160; Our income tax expense recorded in the future will be reduced to the extent of decreases in our valuation allowances.&#160; While we can utilize net operating loss carry-forwards to reduce our Federal income taxes, we will be subject to alternative minimum tax charges.&#160; Changes in the tax laws and rates could also affect recorded deferred tax assets and liabilities in the future.&#160; We are not aware of any such changes that would have a material effect on our results of operations, cash flows or financial position.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Financial Instruments.</font>&#160; Warrants to acquire our common stock that we issued in connection with services provided to us related to an offering of our stock has been valued separately using the Black-Scholes methodology.&#160; The determination of the values attributed to the warrant required the use of estimates and judgments particularly related to the assumptions used in the Black-Scholes calculation.&#160; In addition, options and warrants to acquire common stock issued to employees, directors and consultants have been valued using a Black-Scholes calculation and their valuations are impacted by the assumptions used in this calculation.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Stock-Based Compensation Expense.&#160; </font>We apply the fair value method of accounting for all of our employee stock&#8209;based compensation.&#160; We use the Black&#8209;Scholes option pricing model to determine the fair value of stock option awards at the date of the issuance of the award.&#160; The value is expensed over the vesting period, which is generally three years. See Note 8 to our Annual Consolidated Financial Statements included in this Annual Report on Form 10-K for required disclosures.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Our estimates of stock-based compensation expense require a number of complex and subjective assumptions, including our stock price volatility, employee exercise patterns, future forfeitures, dividend yield, related tax effects and the selection of an appropriate fair value model.&#160; In addition, we have estimated volatility on shares issued in the year ended March 31, 2017 based on an average volatility of a set of companies we consider comparable to us. We use historical data to estimate pre-vesting forfeitures, and we record stock-based compensation expense only for those awards that are expected to vest.&#160; The dividend yield assumption is based on our history and future expectations of dividend payouts.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">The assumptions used in calculating the fair value of stock-based compensation expense and related tax effects represent management&#8217;s best estimates, but these estimates involve inherent uncertainties and the application of management judgment.&#160; As a result, if factors change and we use different assumptions, or if we decide to use a different valuation model, our stock-based compensation expense could be materially different in the future from what we have recorded in the current period, which could materially affect our results of operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: justify; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Foreign Currency Transactions. </font>We regularly enter into transactions that are settled in a foreign currency.&#160; These transactions are recorded in U.S. dollars based on the exchange rate in effect at the time a transaction is initiated.&#160; When a transaction is settled, the foreign currency received to settle the transaction is converted to U.S. dollars based on the exchange rate in effect at the time of settlement.&#160; We record a realized foreign currency exchange gain or loss based on the difference in the exchange rate in effect when a transaction is initiated, and the exchange rate in effect when a transaction is settled.&#160;</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Recent Accounting Pronouncements</div>

<div style="BACKGROUND-COLOR: #ffffff">
<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">For a summary of recent accounting pronouncements applicable to our consolidated financial statements see Note&#160;1 to the Consolidated Financial Statements in Item&#160;8, which is incorporated herein by reference.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Results of Operations</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Revenue.</font>&#160; We derive revenue from sales of our rugged wireless tablet PC systems, which encompass a line of active pen and touch tablet PC computers, embedded wireless, desktop, vehicle, fork-lift or truck docking stations and a range of supporting performance-matched accessories, peripherals and support services.&#160; Our revenue also includes service revenue derived from out-of-warranty repairs and from separately priced extended warranty contracts which is deferred and recognized in income on a straight-line basis over the related contract period.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Cost of Revenue.</font>&#160; Cost of revenue consists of the costs associated with manufacturing, assembling and testing our products, related overhead costs, maintenance, compensation, freight and other costs related to manufacturing support, including depreciation of tooling assets and logistics.&#160; We use contract manufacturers to manufacture our products and supporting components, which represents a significant majority of our cost of revenue.&#160; In addition, the costs associated with providing warranty repairs, as well as the costs associated with generating service revenue, are included in cost of revenue.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Gross Profit.</font>&#160; Gross profit has been, and will continue to be, affected by a variety of factors, including competition, product mix and average selling prices of products, maintenance, new product introductions and enhancements, the cost of components and manufacturing labor, fluctuations in manufacturing volumes, component shortages, the mix of distribution channels through which our products are sold, and warranty costs.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Sales, Marketing and Support.</font>&#160; Sales, marketing and support expenses include salaries, commissions, agent fees and costs associated with co-operative marketing programs, as well as other personnel-related costs, travel expenses, advertising programs, trade shows and other promotional activities associated with the marketing and selling of our products.&#160; We also believe part of our future success will be dependent upon establishing and maintaining successful relationships with a variety of resellers.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Product Research, Development and Engineering.</font>&#160; Product research, development and engineering expenses consist of salaries and related expenses for development and engineering personnel, and non-recurring engineering costs, including prototype costs, related to the design, development, testing and enhancement of our product lines.&#160; We expense our research and development costs as they are incurred.&#160; There may be components of our research and development efforts that require significant expenditures, the timing of which can cause quarterly fluctuation in our expenses.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">General Administration.</font>&#160; General administration expenses consist of salaries and related expenses for finance, accounting, procurement and information technology personnel, investor relations, professional fees, including legal fees for litigation defense and litigation settlement payments, corporate expenses, and costs associated with being a U.S. public company, including regulatory compliance costs.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Interest.</font>&#160; Interest expense includes interest on borrowings or transaction processing fees related to our credit facility.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Other Income and Expense.</font>&#160; Other income and expense includes gains and/or losses on dispositions of assets, gains and losses from currency fluctuations and other miscellaneous income and expense.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Stock Based Compensation.</font>&#160; We recorded stock-based compensation expense in the employee related functional classification.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: justify; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Inflation.</font>&#160; During the fiscal years ended March 31, 2017 and 2016, we believe inflation and changing prices have not had material impact on our revenue or on our net loss from continuing operations.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Foreign Currency Transactions. </font>We regularly enter into transactions that are settled in a foreign currency.&#160; We record these transactions in U.S. dollars based on the exchange rate in effect at the time a transaction is initiated.&#160; When a transaction is settled, the foreign currency received to settle the transaction is converted to U.S. dollars based on the exchange rate in effect at the time of settlement.&#160; A realized foreign currency exchange gain or loss is recorded based on the difference in the exchange rate in effect when a transaction is initiated, and the exchange rate in effect when a transaction is settled.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt"><br>
</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 10pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 10pt; CLEAR: both" id="DSPFPageBreakArea">

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; FONT-STYLE: italic; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt; FONT-WEIGHT: bold">Fiscal Year Ended March&#160;31, 2017 vs. Fiscal Year Ended March&#160;31, 2016</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Revenue.</font>&#160;&#160;Total revenue for the fiscal year ended March&#160;31, 2017 was $77,928,000, compared to $100,530,000 for the fiscal year ended March&#160;31, 2016, representing a decrease of $22,602,000, or approximately 22%.&#160; The decrease in our revenue is attributed to both a decrease in unit sales of approximately 12% and a lower average selling price per unit of approximately 11%.&#160; We believe that in fiscal year 2016, we benefited greatly from the resolution of supply chain issues experienced by Motion prior to the closing of the Motion Acquisition in April 2015.&#160; We believe that this resolution resulted in a surge in shipments of pent-up demand to former Motion customers in fiscal 2016, which did not recur in fiscal year 2017. As a result, we experienced a decrease in unit sales and revenue in fiscal 2017.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">We operate in one segment, the sale of rugged mobile wireless tablet PC computing systems.&#160; Approximately 73% of our revenue in the year ended March 31, 2017 was derived from sales in the United States.&#160;&#160;The United States accounted for approximately 68% of our revenue during the year ended March&#160;31, 2016.&#160; At March&#160;31, 2017, we had a distributor customer, Synnex Information Technologies Inc., who had receivable balances accounting for approximately 11% of our total outstanding accounts receivables.&#160;&#160;We collected the receivable balance from that customer subsequent to our fiscal year end.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">In any year, a single customer may account for a significant portion of our sales.&#160;&#160;For the fiscal year ended March 31, 2017, we had two customers located in the United States who accounted for approximately 20% and 16% of our total revenue, respectively.&#160;&#160;For the fiscal year ended March&#160;31, 2016, we had two customers located in the United States who accounted for approximately 23% and 11% of our total revenue, respectively.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Cost of Revenue.</font>&#160;&#160;Total cost of revenue for the year ended March&#160;31, 2017 was $55,956,000, compared to $69,183,000 for the year ended March&#160;31, 2016, representing a decrease of $13,227,000, or approximately 19%.&#160;&#160;This decrease in cost of revenues is primarily attributable to the decrease in revenue discussed above and a change in the product mix we sold during the periods.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">During the years ended March 31, 2017 and 2016, we relied on three suppliers for the majority of our finished goods.&#160;&#160;The inventory purchases and cost of engineering services from our suppliers during the years ended March&#160;31, 2017 and 2016 were $37,812,000 and $48,017,000, respectively.&#160;&#160;At March&#160;31, 2017 and 2016, we owed these suppliers $6,237,000 and $6,606,000, respectively, which we recorded in accounts payable and accrued liabilities.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Gross Profit.</font>&#160;&#160;Total gross profit decreased by $9,375,000, to $21,972,000 (28.2% of revenue) for the year ended March&#160;31, 2017 from $31,347,000 (31.2% of revenue) for the year ended March&#160;31, 2016.&#160;&#160;The decrease in gross profit for the year ended March&#160;31, 2017, as compared to the prior year, was mainly attributable to the decrease in revenue.&#160; The decrease in the gross profit percentage was due to a different product mix we sold and significant large orders that we sold at discounts during fiscal 2017.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Sales, Marketing and Support Expenses.</font>&#160;&#160;Sales, marketing and support expenses for the year ended March&#160;31, 2017 were $12,370,000, compared to $15,096,000 for the year ended March&#160;31, 2016, a decrease of $2,726,000, or approximately 18%.&#160; The decrease in these expenses was primarily due to a decrease in headcount related costs of $1,821,000, associated with lower commissions and personnel changes, a decrease in the consumption of demo units of $1,054 and a decrease in various other expenses of $273,000, which was partially offset by an increase in marketing related expenses of $402,000.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">Product Research, Development and Engineering Expenses.</font>&#160;&#160;Product research, development and engineering expenses for the year ended March 31, 2017 were $4,462,000, as compared to $5,771,000 for the year ended March 31, 2016, a decrease of $1,309,000, or approximately 23%.&#160; The decrease in these expenses was related to a decrease in headcount expenses of $601,000, a decrease in product development related expenses of $658,000, and a decrease in various other expenses of $50,000.</div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: left; MARGIN-TOP: 3pt; TEXT-INDENT: 36pt; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 6pt; COLOR: #000000; FONT-SIZE: 10pt"><font style="FONT-STYLE: italic; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 10pt">General Administration Expenses.</font>&#160;&#160;General administration expenses for the year ended March&#160;31, 2017 were $7,193,000, compared to $9,427,000 for the year ended March&#160;31, 2016, a decrease of $2,234,000, or approximately 24%.&#160;&#160;The decrease primarily consisted of a decrease in stock-based compensation expense of $1,548,000, primarily due to the absence of a large grant of options to members of our board of directors and certain of our officers in fiscal 2016, a decrease in bad debt expense of $291,000, a decrease in officer bonus accruals of $248,000, a decrease in computer maintenance expense of $199,000, a decrease in rent expense of $153,000 and a decrease in various other expenses of $380,000which was partially offset by an increase in professional expenses of $184,000, an increase in depreciation expense of $105,000 and an increase in various other expenses of $48,000.</div>

<div style="MARGIN-TOP: 10pt; MARGIN-BOTTOM: 10pt; CLEAR: both" id="DSPFPageBreakArea">
<div id="DSPFPageFooter"></div>

<div style="TEXT-ALIGN: center" id="DSPFPageNumberArea"><font style="FONT-STYLE: normal; FONT-FAMILY: 'Times New Roman', Times, serif; FONT-SIZE: 8pt; FONT-WEIGHT: normal" id="DSPFPageNumber">27</font></div>
